illicit affairs

by folksmore

Summary

rio vidal works as famous actress agatha harkness' stylist. she's also her controversially younger affair.

Chapter 1: what started in beautiful rooms

Chapter Summary

The suite was unlike anything Rio had ever stayed in. She imagines that a one night stay there costs at least two months worth of her rent. She never thought she'd spend a night in a place like this, let alone having sex with Agatha Harkness. 
She leans against the door to remove her heels, watching as Agatha does the same from across the room. Her fingers twitch slightly. She can't believe she was actually doing this. 
Or well, actually, she does. She had, somehow, known from the second she met Agatha that she'd end up in bed with her. She just didn't want to admit it. Call it lesbian intuition or whatever.

When Rio Vidal submitted her application for the recently opened position as Agatha Harkness' stylist, she wasn't really expecting to get an interview. Despite her great credentials, Agatha Harkness was still the best actress of her generation. Not only that, but she was always being featured in fashion magazines for her red carpet looks — so much so that Rio was shocked to see the position suddenly open. So, when she got an email back, she figured it was just a courtesy rejection. Instead, a week later, she was being led through her house for a personal interview with Agatha herself.

Agatha's assistant knocks once and pushes the two big wooden doors that led to Agatha's home office open. “Mrs. Harkness, your appointment is here.”

Agatha is sitting comfortably on a leather chair behind her desk, flipping absentmindedly through a thick script. She looks up as Rio is led in and drops the script onto the table.

“Good luck,” her assistant says before leaving the room and closing the door behind her.

“Rio Vidal,” she muses.

Rio swallows thickly and forces a smile. “Yes, Mrs. Harkness, it's an honor to—”

Agatha tsks. “None of that. It's Agatha.”

Rio nods. “Agatha.”

Agatha smiles as her gaze drags over Rio. “Well, aren't you something?”

“Thank you?”

“That was a compliment, darling,” Agatha chuckles.

Rio nods again and clears her throat. 

“Sit,” Agatha says, nodding at the chair across from hers.

“So, you're a FIT girl,” she says as soon as Rio sits down, looking at the resume in front of her. “Graduated at the very top of your class. That's not an easy feat.”

“It wasn't. I worked really hard.”

Agatha smiles approvingly. “Can I see your portfolio?”

“Yes, of course,” Rio replies, taking a thick folder out of her bag and handing it over. 

Agatha flips through it for a while before looking up at Rio. “Your work is very impressive.”

“Thank you.”

“No, I mean it. This is one of the best portfolios I've seen in a while.”

“I work really hard.”

“You minored in… fashion theory?”

Rio nods. “I studied how you can style someone to shape the public perception of them.”

Agatha props her chin onto her hand and arches an eyebrow. “So, what you're saying is that you can use outfits to manipulate someone?”

“It's more of a control thing. It's, like... if you want to be in control, you can be.”

An amused smirk spreads through Agatha's lips as she nods. “I like that.”

Rio hates how much her stomach flips at that.

“Do you like control, Rio?”

“Yes,” she blurts out. Agatha's smirk widens and Rio clears her throat. “I mean, it's useful. In fashion.”

Agatha's gaze drops to her mouth for a few seconds and Rio clears her throat again. What was this woman doing? What was Rio doing? 

“Can I see your references?”

Rio nods. “Of course.” 

Agatha hums as she takes the envelope that Rio pulled out of her bag and gently rips it open.

“It's a little bit long,” Rio says, to which Agatha raises an eyebrow. “Um. Not that it's a bad thing.”

One of the most promising stylists I've mentored,” Agatha begins. “Talent beyond her years.” She looks up and smiles.

“That's quite a reputation.”

“Thank you.”

“You don't know how to react to compliments, do you?”

“No, I just—”

“Do you have your sketchbook with you?” 

“My—my sketchbook?”

Agatha nods. “Your resume also mentions that you designed a lot in the past. I'd like to see it.”

Rio hesitates but still looks through her bag for her sketchbook, handing it out. She inhales sharply when Agatha's fingers brush hers for a second too long. 

“It's more of a side thing now. I still do it sometimes, just for myself.”

Agatha smiles before getting up and circling the desk, perching herself on the edge closest to Rio and opening the sketchbook across her legs. Rio can't help but stare at her legs for a little too long before looking back up.

“You're nervous,” Agatha points out, meeting her gaze.

Rio tries to pretend she hadn't noticed how Agatha's gaze flickered to the lesbian bracelet she was wearing, and then up to her mouth again.

“I'm not,” Rio replies. Too quickly. Agatha smiles, still looking at her lips. “I just wasn't expecting you to want to see my sketches.”

“Mhm.” 

Agatha stops at a rough sketch of a sparkling dark green dress with a high slit and taps her fingernail against it. “This one is especially beautiful.”

“It's not even finished.”

“It's stunning.”

Rio nods. “Thank you.”

“You know, I usually have someone do the interviews for me. I'm glad I decided to interview you myself. Your work is exceptional.”

“Thank—”

“You're hired.”

“Wait, what? Just like that?”

“Would you rather I make you beg?”

“No!” she blurts out. “I just thought—I didn't think it'd be this quick.”

Agatha smirks. “I know what I want when I see it.”

Before Rio can reply, she's interrupted by the door bursting open and a little boy running in. Agatha's son, she presumes. He almost trips on the bunny plushie he was dragging by the ears as he runs toward Agatha.

“Mama!”

Agatha sighs. “Nicky, love, what are you doing out of bed?”

He ignores her, turning toward Rio instead. “Who's this?”

Agatha sits him on her lap and kisses the top of his head. “This is Rio. She's going to be my new stylist.”

“What about Miss Wanda?”

“Wanda isn't coming back.”

“Why?”

“Because she doesn't want to.”

“I liked her,” he pouts.

“I know,” she says softly, stroking his hair. “But you'll like Miss Rio too. Won't he?”

Rio nods quickly, though she was caught off-guard. “Yeah. Of course.”

He clutches his plushie tighter. “Are you good?”

“At my job?”

He nods.

“I guess I am, for your mom to hire me.”

He nods again and squints at her. “Do you like bunnies?”

Rio blinks. “Uh—yes. Bunnies are cool.”

“What's your favorite breed of bunnies?” he beams.

Rio freezes at that question and makes a mental note to research bunny breeds later.

“Alright, Nicky, that's enough for today. Go back to bed.”

“But—”

“Now, Nicholas.”

“Okay, okay,” he mutters quietly, sliding off her lap. 

He stops in front of Rio and tilts his head. “Can you fix him?”

Rio frowns. “What?”

“Señor Scratchy,” he says, holding up his plushie so that Rio could see the small rip in one of its ears. 

“I could stitch him up for you, yeah,” she replies. “Wouldn't even take long.”

“You can?”

Rio looks up at Agatha, who shrugs, before nodding.

Nicky tilts his head again, analyzing her for a few more seconds. “I guess you're cool.”

He once again almost trips on Señor Scratchy as he runs out of the room, and Agatha shakes her head and sighs. 

“Do you like children?”

Rio nods. “I helped raise my younger siblings. I'm pretty good with kids.”

“He can be a lot sometimes,” Agatha smiles.

“I don't mind.”

“If you tell him that, he'll latch onto you.”

Rio smiles. “That's fine by me.”

“So,” she drawls. “Do you need time to think?”

Rio shakes her head again. “No. I'm in.”

“Great. You'll start on Monday,” she says. “I'll arrange for you to meet Wanda Maximoff today. She's my former stylist and will brief you on my preferences. My assistant will send you the details later.”

“Thank you. For hiring me. It's really going to be an honor to work for you.”

Agatha hums and extends her hand, and Rio hesitates a little before taking it. Agatha shakes her hand lightly before sliding off the desk and walking toward the door.

“Oh, and Rio?” she says, glancing over her shoulder. 

Rio looks up.

“You don't have to be that nervous around me. I don't bite.” A pause. “Unless you ask me to.”

“I—”

“My assistant will see you out shortly,” Agatha smiles. 

Until then, Rio is stuck wondering what the fuck she had just gotten herself into.

 

She meets Wanda Maximoff in a small café downtown a few hours later. She was already sitting in a corner booth and scrolling absentmindedly through her phone when Rio arrived.

“Wanda Maximoff? I'm Rio Vidal, Agatha Harkness' new stylist,” she says, extending her hand.

“Took you long enough,” Wanda replies, not bothering to look up at her.

Rio pulls her hand back and checks her watch. She was five minutes early, but she decides not to argue.

“Thank you for meeting me,” she says as she sits down across from Wanda.

“It's not like I had a choice.”

Rio clears her throat and nods.

“Alright. Let's make this quick,” Wanda says dryly, placing a thick folder on the table. “This is a collection of past looks, notes in general, her favorite designers…”

She explains a few things as she flips through the folder. Agatha loves purple, except when it has patterns. Patterns are only acceptable in green, actually — except for florals, because she hates them no matter what. Allergic to wool. She had so many peculiarities that Rio is dizzy by the time Wanda flips to the last page.

“Do you have any other questions, or am I free to go?” 

“Um. I know it's none of my business, but why did you quit?”

A beat.

“I mean, you quit, right? She said that—”

“Harkness says a lot of things,” she replies flatly. 

Rio blinks.

Wanda sighs. “Look. There's one more thing you have to know about Agatha Harkness.”

“Okay.”

“There's nothing in this world that she enjoys more than fucking her stylists. She always has, and always will.”

Rio bursts out laughing, but Wanda's expression doesn't change.

Rio snorts. “You're joking.”

Wanda holds her gaze until she stops laughing.

“She has a husband.”

Wanda raises an eyebrow. “And she also has an assistant who's an expert in booking hotel rooms under different names and the money to sue the life out of whoever threatens to expose her little scheme.”

“He—”

"Doesn't know. Frankly, he's an idiot. And he travels for work all the time, so it's not like he's around to keep her in check. When he's around, she's the perfect wife and mother. People don't question it because she doesn't give anyone any reason to.”

“Okay, but that’s not why she hired me.”

Wanda smirks. “I don't doubt that she liked your portfolio, because from what I've heard you're extremely talented, but I think that she liked your face even more. Young and insanely pretty. You're exactly her type.”

“Thank you, I guess,” Rio frowns. “Should I—”

“She won't ever make you do anything you don't want to do, if that's what you're worried about. She's not like that. But she knows how to make you want her.”

“I don't even know why you're telling me all this. You don't know if I'm into women or—”

Wanda scoffs and points at Rio's bracelet. “Well, you're not exactly subtle about it.”

Right.

A beat. Then, Rio asks, “How long did you two… you know.”

“Two years, on and off.” 

“And before you?”

“Let's just say I wasn't the first, Rio. And I certainly won't be the last.”

“Did anyone know about the two of you?”

Wanda shrugs. “Some of my friends, yes. I wouldn't tell them everything, but they knew that it was happening. Her assistant and her driver definitely know. As long as they don't say anything to the media, which they have no reason to do, Harkness doesn't really give a shit.”

Rio swallows thickly. She hesitates before asking, “Did you love her?”

Wanda looks down at her cup and sighs. That was the only answer she needed.

“She makes you feel special. And when she looks at you the way that she does, you'll be convinced that you're different and that she actually cares about you and not about the sex.”

“And why did you finally decide to leave?”

“Has anyone ever told you that you ask a lot of questions?”

“I'm sorry, I just—”

Wanda exhales sharply and gets up. “If you want some advice, just keep your head down and do your job. Best case scenario, she'll see you're not interested and fire you. And don't think that you're any different, because you aren't.”

Rio nods. 

“And if I were you, I wouldn't wear that around Ralph,” Wanda says, pointing at her bracelet.

She grabs her bag and swings it over her shoulder without waiting for an answer. “Anyways. Good luck to you. You'll need it.”

“Thanks, I guess,” she says quietly, but Wanda had already left.

 

Rio kicks off her heels as she walks into the apartment that she shared with her childhood best friends. She met Alice Gulliver and Jennifer Kale in first grade, and they had been inseparable ever since, having moved in together during college.

“And? How did it go?” Alice asks, immediately jumping up from the couch.

“Which part? The one where Agatha Harkness hired me on the spot or—”

Not surprisingly, she gets interrupted by Alice and Jen shrieking and jumping around her. She exhales sharply and holds up her hand, which somehow works and makes them stop yelling.

“Or the part where her former stylist told me that I just signed up to be Agatha Harkness' next fuck toy?”

Silence. Then, in unison, “Wait, what?”

Rio sinks into the couch with a groan, and then spends the next half an hour telling them about the interview and about her meeting with Wanda. She only left out the less important parts, such as Agatha saying she could bite her if Rio asked. It'd undoubtedly have been so much quicker if Alice and Jen wouldn't have kept interrupting her to either gasp dramatically or to ask a random question.

“Okay, pause,” Alice says. “You're telling us that your insanely hot and rich boss is actively looking to cheat on her unsuspecting husband with you?” 

“That's not what I said at all. Listen, I'm not even sure if I believe it. It might all be an elaborate prank to mess with her new stylist, for all I know.” 

“And if it isn't a prank, what's your plan?” Jen asks.

Rio looks at her like she had just asked the most obvious question in the world. “To do my job and not fuck my married boss.”

Alice and Jen exchange a glance and burst out laughing.

“What? This isn't funny.”

“You expect us to believe that you, Rio Vidal, will turn down a chance to get laid?” Alice laughs. 

“I do have some self-control, you know.”

“Must I remind you of your body count in college?” 

“First of all, fuck you. Second, it doesn't mean I'll fuck my married boss.”

“It's not our fault that you have a track record,” Jen shrugs.

“I had some fun in college,” Jen laughs. “You fucked over half of the women in your class. And a professor.”

“The professor was a one time thing.”

“You still ended up on her desk.”

“No, I didn't.” She pauses. “Okay, one time was on her desk.” Another pause. She clears her throat. “A few times were on her desk. It doesn't matter, okay? None of these women were married, which was my whole point.”

“No. Even if I was interested, which I'm not—”

Jen raises an eyebrow. “You're not?”

Rio groans. “Okay, she's, like, insanely hot. If she wasn't married, maybe I'd fuck her. Maybe.”

“That's not a no,” Alice says.

“Yes, it is. I'm not a homewrecker. That's a line I won't ever cross.”

Alice and Jen exchange a look and chuckle.

“I wouldn't blame you if you did,” Jen says. “I'd totally fuck Agatha Harkness if she thought that I was hot.”

“She doesn't think that I'm hot.”

“Of course not. She was just staring at your mouth because she was interested in what shade of lipstick you were wearing.”

Rio rolls her eyes. “Seriously now, I know that you two are finding this really funny, but it has to stay between us. No one can know that this is going on.”

Rio shoots her a glare. “With her past stylists, yeah. I'm not falling for it.”

“Okay, but—”

“I mean it. I need you two to promise me that you'll keep your mouths shut.”

Jen snorts. “Do you think I'd risk getting sued by Agatha Harkness for telling someone that she likes to have some fun outside of her marriage?”

Rio sighs and runs her hands through her face. “I need some sleep. Today was insane.”

As she walks toward her room, she hears them whisper something and laugh, so she slams her door shut for good measure. 

Hours later, she was still awake. She only slept after she finished the sketch Agatha had liked, much later that night.

She has a slight feeling that she's fucked, and that she'll be fucking Agatha Harkness sooner rather than later, but she shakes that last thought off. Agatha was one of the hottest women she had ever come across, sure, but she was also married. Rio would never risk ruining a family for some obviously casual sex. Or at least she was fairly certain about that.

 

Monday came by way too fast. Before Rio knew it, she was being led through Agatha's house and into her office again. Agatha's assistant knocks twice before opening the door and waving her in.

This time, Agatha is in the company of a man, who Rio recognizes as Ralph Bohner. She doesn't know much about him other than the fact that he was Agatha's idiot husband who was rarely around. That and the fact that he had a major investment company, according to LinkedIn. She hadn't stalked his profile, of course, having spent just around half an hour snooping through it with a burner account.

He looks up as soon as she steps into the room, and so Rio quickly pulls her sleeves down, covering her bracelet. She curses herself for forgetting that she's not supposed to be wearing it here. 

“Ralph, this is Rio Vidal,” Agatha says. 

Rio swallows hard and forces herself to smile. “Mr. Bohner.”

“Just Ralph is fine,” he smiles.

“Ralph,” she nods.

“So you're the new girl my wife hasn't stopped talking about.”

Rio blinks. “I—she hasn't?”

“Agatha has been obsessed with you,” he says. “At one point, I had to ask myself if I should be worried.”

Rio chuckles and looks up at Agatha, who winks at her. 

“Seriously. She hasn't stopped talking about you. It's always ‘Ralph, you have to see her work’, ‘Ralph, she'll be the best stylist I ever had’,” he laughs. “And she's had a lot of stylists, so that's quite the compliment.”

“That's really nice of her.”

“Friday?” 

“Saturday. Jeff arranged a last minute meeting with some investors on Friday night, so we had to change our flight.”

“I hate it when you leave,” Agatha pouts.

“You love the money, though.”

“Touché.”

Ralph leans in and presses a lingering kiss to her lips, which makes Rio scan the floor until she finds a particularly interesting stain to stare at. 

“I'll call you when I land,” he says, walking toward the door and stopping by Rio on his way out. “Nice meeting you, Rio.”

“Likewise,” Rio nods.

“And take care of my wife. Make sure she behaves herself while I'm gone.”

Rio stiffens slightly. “Yes, of course.”

Her gaze shifts to Agatha, who rolls her eyes and waved a hand dismissively.

“God, I thought he'd never leave,” Agatha groans as soon as he closes the door behind him.

Rio raises an eyebrow. “You make it sound like he's a burden.”

“Men are always a burden,” Agatha laughs. 

“Even the one you married?”

“Especially the one I married.”

Rio doesn't know what to say to that, so she only nods. 

“Who does he think he is to tell me that I love the money? For fuck's sake. I can make more money with two interviews than what his company makes in a whole year.”

‘I—”

“That's—”

“You can dump shit on me,” Rio blurts out.

Agatha smirks as she meets her gaze. “Yeah?” 

“I mean—you can talk to me. I guess.”

“You covered your bracelet,” Agatha points out.

“Um. Yeah. Wanda said—”

“I know what she said,” Agatha says, reaching for her wrist and pulling Rio's sleeve back up. “You don't really have to worry about it. He's not smart enough to know what it means.”

Rio nods and clears her throat. “Anyways. So. Work?”

“Eager, darling?”

“What? No. I just—I assume that we have a lot to do.”

Agatha studies for a second before chuckling. “Right. Follow me.”

She barely holds back a gasp when they finally reach Agatha's closet. It had to be almost the size of Rio's apartment, and it was filled with designer clothes, shoes and purses that Rio knows cost more than makes in a year. Maybe two, for some of them. 

“And?” Agatha says, raising an eyebrow.

“This is insane.”

“I believe the word you're looking for is impressive.”

“Insanely impressive, yes.”

“Well, as you know, I have a reputation to maintain. And now it's your job to help me do that.”

“Of course.”

Her first day started simple enough and mostly consisted of planning outfits for Agatha's next appearances. She quickly learns that the peculiarities Wanda had told her about only added up to one percent of the real deal. Not that she really cared, given that she was quickly learning that she quite loved being near Agatha. Not that it means anything other than Agatha being a good boss that she doesn't mind working with. The hardest part of her job was making sure that her fingers wouldn't linger too long when adjusting pieces on Agatha's body, which Rio was sure also doesn't mean anything. 

Nearing the end of their workday, for some unknown reason, as she's adjusting a silk blouse on Agatha's shoulder, Rio clears her throat and asks, “Is it true what Ralph said about you?”

Agatha turns her head, her mouth dangerously close to Rio's. “Which part?”

Rio instinctively takes a step back, almost stumbling over her own feet as she does. “That you couldn't stop talking about me.”

She smirks. “Why do you want to know?”

“Just curious.”

“I do think you're fascinating, darling.”

“Should I be worried?”

Agatha ignores that question. “What else did Wanda tell you?”

“A lot about your styling preferences.”

“And?”

“Nothing else.”

Agatha scoffs. “I don't believe that for a second. Wanda has quite the mouth on her when she wants to. I imagine that she has a few opinions about me.”

“I don't know what you're talking about.”

“Would you care if anything that she said was true?

“Honestly, Agatha, I do care that you seem to think I'd ever be a homewrecker.”

Agatha raises an eyebrow and stays quiet for the rest of the session. Good. 

Rio is completely exhausted by the time they are done. All she wants is to go home, take a warm shower and scream into her pillow, and then maybe send her very nice boss her two week notice.

“Before you go,” Agatha says. “Nicky hasn't stopped talking about how you promised to fix his plushie.”

Right. Agatha had a kid. And also a husband who's the father of said kid, Rio reminds herself.

She scoffs. “You could do it yourself, you know. Or is operating a sewing needle too much for you?”

Agatha raises an eyebrow and chuckles. 

“Sorry. I didn't mean—”

“No. You're bold. I like that.”

Silence.

“Anyways. He wants you to fix it. I'll call him over for you.”

Agatha leaves before Rio can even nod. She decides to take the chance to Google bunny breeds while Agatha is gone, just in case Nicky asks her about it again. She comes back a while later, holding Nicky's hand. He's clutching Señor Scratchy tightly with his other hand, and Rio notices that the tear looks a bit worse than the last time she saw him. 

“Go on, Nicky. Tell Miss Rio what you want.”

He walks toward her, dragging the plushie behind him. “You said you'd fix him. So fix him.”

“Nicholas, say please,” Agatha says firmly.

Rio shakes her head. “Agatha, it's fine.”

“No, it's not,” Agatha says. “Nicholas, say please.”

“Please?”

Agatha runs a hand through his hair and kisses the top of his head. “See? Not hard.”

Rio holds out her hand and Nicky carefully hands Señor Scratchy over, watching as she gets up and fishes her bag for her sewing kit. As promised, she fixes the tear in a matter of minutes. 

Nicky gasps as she hands the plushie back over. “How did you fix him that fast?”

“I went to school for this sort of thing,” she smiles. 

“They don't teach me this sort of thing at my school!”

Agatha puts a hand on his shoulder. “Now say thank you, Nicky.”

“Thank you!”

“And Nicky?” Rio says softly, kneeling next to him. 

“Yeah?”

“I really like Holland Lops.”

He beams. “Me too! I like you a lot already, Miss Rio!”

Rio looks at Agatha, who simply shrugs, and then watches as Nicky runs out of the room without another word.

“Wow. Impressing me, impressing my son… congratulations. Not an easy feat.”

“Thanks. I guess?”

“You'll really have to learn how to take compliments, though,” she chuckles.

She's halfway down the hall when she hears a loud sob behind her.

“Miss Rio?”

She turns around to see Nicky clutching Señor Scratchy and wiping his face with his free hand, though it isn't doing much, given how hard he's crying.

“Sweetheart, what are you doing here?” she asks, doing her best to keep her voice from cracking. 

She approaches him and kneels down to run her hands through his hair.

“Are you fighting because of me?” he asks through a choked sob.

“No, no, no, no, no,” Rio assures him. She pulls him into a tight hug and he immediately buries his head in her shoulder. “This isn't because of you, buddy. At all. You didn't do anything wrong.”

“But I told daddy about the KitKat,” he chokes out. “I forgot it was a secret. And that's why daddy got so angry all of sudden, and—I didn't mean to tell on you, I swear!”

“Hey. You were just being honest, okay? And it's never bad to be honest about something.”

“But I didn't mean to,” he sobs. “I'm sorry.”

Nicky flinches slightly when Ralph raises his voice. Oh, how Rio hates this man. She hugs him tighter and cradles his face, covering his little ears.

“Shh, it's okay. You're okay. I've got you.”

“Why won't they stop?” he sobs into her shoulder.

“Do you know how sometimes you disagree with your friends at school, and then you apologize to them and everything is alright?”

Nicky nods into her shoulder.

“That's what's going to happen to mommy and daddy, okay?” 

He pulls back and sniffles. “Do you promise?”

Rio isn't sure if she can actually promise that, but she'd do anything to make Nicky stop crying.

“Yeah, sweetheart, I promise. I know it's scary, but they'll stop soon, okay?”

She wipes the last of his tears with her thumbs and kisses his forehead. 

“Are you coming back tomorrow?” he asks.

“No, Nicky.”

“But you'll be back next week, right?”

“No, Nicky, I won't. I'm sorry.”

“Are you going to be here for my birthday next month?”

Rio sighs and strokes his hair. “No, I won't, sweetheart.”

“But—why?”

“Because I did a bad thing, and I need to not be here anymore so mommy and daddy can fix it.”

“Is—is daddy going to send mommy away too if they can't fix it?”

“No, no,” she says quickly. “That's not going to happen, okay? They'll talk, and they'll fix it. It's what people who love each other do.”

“But why do you have to leave? Why can't you fix it too?”

“You can't fix everything, Nicky.”

“But…”

Rio closes her eyes and swallows hard. “Do you remember how Miss Wanda left?”

He nods, already tearing up again. Rio has to fight the urge to cry with him.

“That's what I'll be doing too, buddy. I'll find a new person to work with, and so will your mom. But it's not because of you, or because I want to. I would stay here with you forever if I could.”

“And why can't you?”

How could Rio possibly explain homophobia to a five-year-old? She exhales shakily.

“It's… complicated. Really complicated. But sometimes, some people just don't agree with what other people are.”

Nicky frowns. “Why?”

“I wish I knew, sweetheart.” She kisses his forehead again. “I really have to go now, okay?”

As was to be expected, that makes Nicky start sobbing heavily again. 

“You can't go,” he begs. “Please, Miss Rio, I love you so much, and—”

This time, Rio can't hold back her own tears.

“I know. And I love you so much too, buddy. And I'll keep loving you, even if I can't come see you anymore.”

Nicky sniffles hard and fights his way out of Rio's grip. Before she can reach for him again, he runs down the hall back to his bedroom, still sobbing loudly. 

“Nicky…”

He comes back a minute later holding a dog plushie. This one, Rio had learned, was his second favorite plushie after Señor Scratchy. He shoves it in her hands with a quiet sob.

“You need to take Sparky,” he says. “Sparky helps me when I'm feeling sad, and you're really sad, and he's Señor Scratchy's best friend, and you're my best friend, and—”

“Nicky, love,” she says. “I can't take him. Your daddy is going to be so angry when he notices Sparky's gone.”

“I don't care!” he sobs, pushing the plushie further into her hands. “You have to take him. You need him.”

“Nicky… sweetheart. This is so, so kind, but I can't. Sparky is yours.”

“I want you to have him,” he insists. “Please, Miss Rio.”

Rio is pretty sure nothing in the world will make Nicky stop crying this time, but taking Sparky is probably going to help a bit. She hopes he won't get in trouble later. 

“Okay,” she whispers and gently takes the plushie from his hands. “I'll take very good care of him, I promise.”

Nicky nods and wipes at his eyes. “He's going to take really good care of you too. I asked him to.”

Rio reaches for him again and hugs him tighter than before, pulling back after a moment to kiss the top of his head and stroke his hair.

“Can you be really brave for me?”

Nicky nods.

“Hey,” she says gently. “I'll need you to take really good care of your mom, okay? And be really good for her.”

He nods again. “Okay.”

She hugs him one last time before standing up and making her way toward the front door.

“Miss Rio,” he calls out. “You won't forget about me, right?”

Rio honestly feels like she's just going to die from sadness. She hasn't felt like this since the night her father died two decades ago. If she really stops to think about it, this might be even worse.

She swallows hard and wipes at her eyes before turning around. “No, baby. Never, never, never. I promise, okay?”

Nicky nods and starts sobbing again as he turns around to run back to his room. If she wasn't painfully aware that Ralph could come out of the bedroom at any moment, she'd have followed him and stayed with him for as long as he needed. 

 

The Uber ride back to her apartment is a total blur. Rio can't really remember how she got into the car, or how she got out of the car, or how she managed to climb the stairs to her apartment. Her legs are weak and her vision is blurry from how much she was crying. She slips inside quietly after struggling with the lock for what felt like ages.

“Hey,” Alice calls from the couch, not bothering to look up from the book she was reading. “What took you so long? I thought you were only going to adjust a dress, and—”

She finally looks up. The look on Rio's tear soaked face makes her drop her book immediately and rush toward her. By the time she gets to the door, Rio has crumpled to her knees and is sobbing uncontrollably, having pulled Sparky close to her chest to hug him tightly.

“Hey, hey, what the hell happened?” Alice asks, pulling Rio close.

When Rio only sobs harder, Alice yells for Jen, who rushes out of her room and toward them.

“Rio, baby,” Jen coos. “Hey. What happened?”

“He knows,” she eventually chokes out. “Ralph knows.”

“Oh, Rio…” Alice whispers. 

She does her best to explain what had happened, though Alice and Jen don't push and just keep reassuring her with things such as this wasn't your fault and you didn't do anything wrong. It doesn't help much, obviously.

Eventually, they get Rio onto her feet and slowly walk her into her room to tuck her into bed. They stay with her until Rio manages to convince them that she's fallen, and then she can hear them having a small discussion outside her door about how no, Jen could not go after Agatha and beat her up, because that would only make everything worse.

 

On the days that follow, Rio barely leaves her bed, and Alice and Jen frequently bring her food and water or just sit there with her in silence. Rio's phone rings insistently during that time, though she never picks up the calls from the multiple unidentified numbers that try to reach her and she eventually puts her phone on airplane mode. Her suspicions about what the calls were about are confirmed a week later when multiple of her friends send her a link to a TMZ article.

 

EXCLUSIVE: AGATHA HARKNESS’ PROMINENT STYLIST RIO VIDAL FIRED

Published by TMZ

An inside source who asked to remain anonymous has informed the TMZ that Rio Vidal, who had been styling Agatha Harkness for over a year, has been let go from her position for unspecified reasons. Rio Vidal is the 6th stylist to part ways with Harkness in the last ten years, having followed rising star Wanda Maximoff (who held the position for two and a half years, the longest time amongst Agatha's stylists).

Rio Vidal also designed for Agatha’s most recent movie Down the Road (dir. by Lilia Calderu), as well as having created multiple dresses for the actress, including the Best Dressed winning piece from this year's Oscars.

We were unable to reach Rio Vidal and Agatha Harkness’ team refused to comment. There is no information on who will be styling the actress for her upcoming appearances.

 

Rio becomes even more of a shadow of herself after that. She starts keeping her bedroom door locked most of the time, and Alice and Jen give up on knocking after the third day. Rio truly feels like she's never going to recover. The days blur quickly, and there are times that Rio isn't sure of how long it has been since she last saw Agatha. It feels like it has been forever. 

She thinks things can't get any worse. But, as the world goes, they do. She wakes up one morning to two messages from Alice.

 

07:34am

I'm so sorry.

[Attachment]

 

Her heart shatters when the page loads.

 

EXCLUSIVE: AGATHA HARKNESS AND RALPH BOHNER SEEN ATTENDING MARRIAGE COUNSELING

Published by TMZ

Multiple award winning actress Agatha Harkness and her husband of ten years, entrepreneur Ralph Bohner, were spotted earlier this week leaving a clinic specialized in marriage counseling. A source close to the couple tells us that they're “working on things and have been doing better since starting therapy a few weeks ago”. The couple has been married for over a decade and share a five-year-old son.

Fans have speculated that this is related to the recent firing of Rio Vidal (and of all of Agatha's short-lived stylists, for that matter) last month, but none of the parties involved have offered comments on the situation. We wish the best for the couple going forward!

 

Rio knows then that she truly will never get Agatha back, and so she will never recover. She convinces herself to at least pretend that she is.

When she finally leaves her room many hours later, she's wearing the shortest dress that she has (or well, second shortest. The shortest is still reserved for Agatha's eyes and Agatha's eyes only) and has done her makeup for the first time since the breakup. Alice and Jen are sitting by the kitchen counter whispering to each other, and Rio can only guess it's about her, especially given how fast their heads snap toward her when she clears her throat. 

“Hi, Rio,” Jen says softly. “How are you doing?”

Rio shrugs. “Can't be doing okay, can I?”

“Yeah, of course,” Jen nods. “I'm sorry. I shouldn't have asked that.”

Rio shrugs again and searches the fridge for a bottle of water. 

“Um, where are you going?” Alice asks carefully.

“Out.”

“Do you want us to go with you?”

Another shrugs. “Whatever. If you want to.”

Alice and Jen look at each other before nodding. 

“Yeah,” Jen says. “Of course. We'll be ready in ten.”

Rio nods with a sigh. Honestly, she wants to go out by herself and just pick a random woman to go home with without the extra company, but they have done so much for her in this past month that she feels like letting them be near her was the least that she can do.

They have been at the bar for a few hours and Rio was quite a few drinks down when she spots a familiar person a few seats ahead. She absolutely should not do what she's about to do, but she also shouldn't have fucked a married woman for over a year, so… 

“I'll be right back,” she says.

Alice and Jen nod.

Wanda Maximoff probably has a radar of some sort, because she turns to face Rio the second she gets three seats closer.

“Rio Vidal,” she says. “Wow.”

“Didn't expect to see you here,” Rio says as she slides into the seat next to her. From the little that she knew about Wanda, she probably should have asked for permission to do that, but whatever.

Wanda's eyes narrow. “You mean on a weekend, at the most popular lesbian bar in the town I live in?”

Rio winces. “Something like that.”

“Though I have to say, I wasn't expecting to see you here either,” Wanda says. “After what happened…”

“I didn't want to believe you when you told me that she likes to fuck her stylists,” Rio says quietly, swirling her drink with the straw.

Wanda scoffs. “Liking is an understatement.”

Rio nods and takes a small sip of her drink.

“How long did it take her to fuck you?” Wanda asks, rather bluntly.

Rio chokes on her drink. 

“What?” Wanda laughs. “It's a completely valid question.”

“A month,” she eventually replies.

Wanda nods. “She moved on faster than I thought she would.”

Wanda seems a little sad. Or just pissed off. Maybe both. Either way, Rio decides to drop the subject. She swirls her drink again and clears her throat.

“So…” Wanda says, raising an eyebrow and tapping her fingers against the table.

It's quite obvious what she wants to know. Rio sighs.

“Ralph found out.”

“That's what I heard.”

“Wasn't pretty.”

“I bet,” she says. Then, after a beat, she adds, “I'm really sorry.”

Rio nods and takes a long sip of her drink before resting her chin on her hand and just… staring. She's obviously quite drunk at this point, and surely that is why Wanda suddenly looks so hot. She doesn't compare to Agatha, obviously, but…

“You're looking at me like you want something,” Wanda says.

“No. I—um. I'm just… looking at you.” 

Wanda chuckles. “Come on, Rio. You're not exactly subtle.”

Rio has a huge feeling she was going to deeply regret what she's about to do, but whatever. She clears her throat and Wanda raises an eyebrow.

“You know, if we were to,” she says, gesturing vaguely, “purely hypothetically, of course, and she heard about it, it would, um…”

Wanda frowns. “Rio, I have no idea what you're talking about.”

“Yes, you do.”

When Wanda cocks her head, Rio takes a deep breath and says, “If we were to fuck. Like… she wouldn't like that, would she?”

Wanda stares at her for a while and then bursts out laughing. When Rio doesn't laugh with her, she stops and stares at her again.

“You're actually serious.”

Rio shrugs. “I'm not not serious.”

“Rio, that's—why?”

“Um. Let's see. I'm hot. You're hot. Like, respectfully, of course. And if this somehow reached her, she wouldn't like it at all. And we're both lonely, and—”

“Bold of you to assume I don't have anyone,” Wanda replies.

Rio freezes. “Oh, fuck. Fuck. I'm sorry. I didn't know you were—fuck. This is so embarrassing. Sorry.”

Wanda chuckles. “I was just messing with you. I'm not seeing anyone.”

“Oh. Right. Of course.”

Rio finishes her drink and Wanda signals the bartender for another. Alright, Rio thinks. Maybe she isn't that much of a monster.

“So… about this plan of yours,” Wanda says. “Do you actually think that I'm hot or are you just trying to get me in bed to get revenge on the witch?”

“The—who?”

“Agatha?” Wanda frowns. “Answer my question.”

“Yeah, I—um. Of course I think that you're hot. Like, really hot, actually.”

“How do you even pull women when you get this flustered over a little question?” Wanda laughs.

“I am not flustered.”

“Alright, then,” she says, pushing her drink aside. “You may kiss me.”

When Rio doesn't make a move, Wanda rolls her eyes and pulls her in by the front of her jacket. Rio gasps against her lips before melting into it, threading her hands in Wanda's hair and pulling her closer. This feels so wrong and so not Agatha. Rio hates it so much.

“Not bad, Vidal,” Wanda chuckles when they pull apart. “I can see why the witch kept you around for that long.”

Whatever. The damage is already done.

“Trust me,” Rio smiles. “You haven't seen anything yet.”

Wanda raises an eyebrow and smirks. 

“Your place?” Rio asks tentatively. “I have roommates.”

When Wanda only laughs, she continues, “I mean. Assuming you want to have sex with me.”

Wanda laughs again, but she gets up. “Thought you'd never ask.”

She throws a bunch of random bills onto the counter and pushes Rio's hand down when she goes to do the same.

“Relax. There's enough there to pay for the whole bar.”

Rio is fucking her to forget Agatha, and she's doing a very Agatha thing right now, which wasn't going to help.

“I should just let my friends know I'm leaving,” she says. “I assume you don't plan on me going back home tonight?”

“As long as you don't plan on disappointing me.”

Rio fights the urge to roll her eyes. This is going to be a long night, and not in a good way, Rio fears. She could just ask Alice and Jen to sneak her out, but… she needs to be with someone else so, so bad.

“Look who's back,” Jen chuckles. “Having fun?”

“Um. Yeah. About that.”

They raise an eyebrow and Rio sighs and nods toward Wanda.

“Before you say anything…”

Jen blinks. “You. Her. Seriously?”

Rio shrugs. “I thought you guys wanted me to have fun. That's all this is.”

Jen and Alice exchange a look. 

“I just need to sleep somewhere else other than in my bed, and she's just… here, and she understands me, and—”

“Okay,” Alice says quickly. “Good for you… I guess?”

“Just be careful, Rio,” Jen says. “Please.”

Rio nods and hugs them goodbye before she can change her mind. Wanda smiles and offers her a hand when Rio approaches her again, and Rio hesitantly takes it to follow her outside and into an Uber.

 

Wanda is still asleep when Rio wakes up the following morning. She runs her hands through her face and gets up with a sigh. Not surprisingly, this didn't fix anything.

She picks her clothes off the floor and gets dressed quickly before using her phone's flashlight to look for a pen and a piece of paper on Wanda's desk. She writes down her number and places the paper on Wanda's bedside table and stares at it for a while before grabbing it again and adding,

 

Text me (if you want).

-- R

 

 

 

She stares at it for a while before groaning and tearing it in half. She puts on her heels and quietly leaves the apartment, throwing the ripped pieces of paper in the first trash can she runs into on her way home. 

Alice and Jen are sitting on the couch with their arms crossed over their chests when Rio arrives.

“How was it?” Jen asks.

“Pointless.” Rio clears her throat. “Anyways. Good morning to you too.”

Alice and Jen exchange a look. 

“Okay. So, um… have you checked the internet today? Like, at all?” Alice asks.

“No…?” 

Alice offers Rio her phone, and on the screen there's a blurry photo of her and Wanda kissing at the bar the previous night. She swipes down to see reports that they were seen leaving together. 

“It's all over Twitter, you know…” Jen says. “People are talking.”

“God, no one even really cares,” Rio groans. “Give it a few hours and everyone will have forgotten about it. I'm not famous. Wanda is not famous. And—”

The three of them are startled by a series of loud knocks at the door. Rio frowns as she goes to unlock it.

She stumbles a little when Agatha pushes past her.

“Are you out of your fucking mind?” Agatha growls. “You did this to get back at me.”

“Okay, hi, absolutely not,” Alice snaps, getting up from the couch and quickly walking toward her. “You do not get to walk into our apartment like that.”

Agatha turns  around. “This is between me and Rio.”

“No, it's not,” Jen says, also getting up to step between the two of them. “Get the hell out, Agatha.”

“Not until she tells me what the fuck she was thinking.”

“What do you want, Agatha?” Rio asks.

“I want to know why the fuck you thought it was okay to fuck Wanda Maximoff of all people. Seriously, Rio? Is this your idea of revenge?”

“You made your choice, Agatha.”

“And this is yours?”

This time, it's Alice who steps between them. 

“You do not have the right to show up here and tell Rio what she can and cannot do,” she says, pointing a finger at her. “Get the fuck out of here.”

“You don't know anything about what's happening.”

“Oh, trust me, we know plenty,” Jen says with a scoff. “We know you kept making Rio bend over backwards for you for months. We know you let her take the fall for what you started. We know you got down on your knees to beg your husband for forgiveness while he was saying whatever he wanted to Rio, and you not once tried to defend her, and—”

“Jen, that's enough,” Rio says quietly.

“No, Rio, it's not enough,” Jen snaps. “It's only going to be enough when this bitch gets out of my apartment and leaves you alone.”

Agatha looks at Rio one more time, her chest heaving. “Rio…”

“Get out, Agatha,” Rio whispers. “I don't want you here.”

“Are you deaf?” Alice hisses, brushing past Agatha and opening the door. “Get. Out.”

When Agatha refuses to budge, Jen curses under her breath and goes to grab her phone from the couch. 

“I'll call the cops,” she says. “And I'll tell them that Agatha Harkness is in my house and refusing to leave. I'm sure Twitter will love that too.”

“No,” Agatha says. “I'll leave.”

She looks at Rio one last time before shaking her head and making her way out. Rio has a huge feeling that she's never going to see Agatha again.

“Rio,” Alice says softly. “Are you okay?”

Rio replies by practically sprinting toward her bedroom and slamming the door shut behind her. She unlocks her phone and types a much needed message through a few tears.

Alice and Jen decide to let her have her alone time, and when she leaves her bedroom again a few hours later, they are halfway done doing the dishes after dinner.

She sinks down onto a chair and sighs. “I need to start over.”

“Okay,” Jen says, approaching her and squeezing her hand.

“Away from here.”

“Wait, what?”

“I texted my mom. She said I can go live with her for a while. And I don't know, maybe I can go back to doing commissions, and—”

“Rio…” Alice says softly. “I mean. If this is what you want… we won't stop you. But are you sure?”

“It's not what I want,” Rio mutters. “But it's definitely what I need. I'm sorry.”

“You don't have to be sorry,” Jen says. “At all. We understand why being here doesn't feel right anymore. And I'm sure that Hela will be so happy to have her little girl back home for a while.”

Rio tries to fight back her tears, but as per usual, that doesn't work. Alice and Jen hug her tightly and take turns kissing the top of her head.

“We'll be right here when you're ready to come back,” Jen says, rubbing soothing circles over her back. “Whenever that is.”

They eventually move to the couch and stay glued to each other for a few hours. After that, they spend the night packing, because Rio has used most of her savings on a very expensive plane ticket for the following evening. 

 

When she arrives in Salem, she tells Hela everything.

She hates herself for thinking that her mom would judge her for it. Instead, Hela holds her and kisses the top of her head until she falls asleep. When Rio wakes up the following morning, Hela has made her her favorite meal from her childhood. For the first time in a while, Rio thinks that maybe, just maybe, it'd be alright.

 

She has been living in Salem for a month when Agatha stops by her apartment. 

“What the fuck are you doing here?” Jen snaps as she opens the door. “Who the fuck do you even think you are?”

“Can I talk to her?” Agatha asks. “Please?”

“Rio isn't here,” Alice says flatly. “Thanks to you.”

Agatha frowns. “When will she be home? I really need to talk to her.”

“No, you don't,” Alice snaps. “Go home, Agatha.” 

“Alice, please. I know I fucked up. I know nothing I can ever do will fix it. But I need to talk to her. Please.”

“Are you fucking deaf, Agatha? We told you, Rio is not here.”

“Where is she?” 

Jen whispers something in Alice's ear and she shrugs.

“She went back to her hometown,” Alice says.

“Wait. What? When?”

“The day after you thought you had the right to come here and make her feel guilty for trying to move on with her life. You have no idea what that did to her.”

“I—fuck. I didn't mean—fuck, I was just so angry that she'd ever do that to me, and—”

Jen scoffs. “After everything you did to her, she gives you a little payback and you act like it's her fault that you couldn't handle it?”

Agatha's throat bobs.

“Get the fuck out of our house, Agatha.”

Before Agatha can say anything else, Alice slams the door on her face and locks it.

Agatha comes back the following day, this time holding a handwritten letter.

“You have to be fucking kidding me,” Jen says as she opens the door. She immediately goes to slam it shut again, but Agatha holds the door open.

“I know you won't give me her address. But can you at least send this to her for me?”

“No,” Jen says flatly. “Get the fuck out of here before we call the cops on you for trespassing.”

“Please.”

“After everything you've done to her, you want to disturb her peace by sending her a letter? Seriously, Agatha?”

“Please,” she says again, quieter this time. “I promise I'll never bother her, or you guys, ever again. I just need her to know some things.”

Alice leans in and whispers something in Jen's ear. Jen sighs and nods once.

“We'll ask her tonight if she'd like to read it. If she does, then we'll send it,” Alice says. 

Agatha nods. “Yes, of course.”

She hands Alice the letter and steps back into the hallway. “Thank you. For helping me.”

“We're not doing this for you,” Jen says curtly. “But you should definitely thank us for not slamming the door on your face the second you showed up here this time.”

“I know.”

Jen grabs the door handle and Agatha barely has the time to take a few steps back before the door is slammed shut.

Later that night, during their nightly FaceTime call, Alice holds the envelope in front of the camera. Rio frowns.

“Okay, we'll make this quick. Agatha came here yesterday. We didn't—”

Predictably, Rio gasps. “She what?”

“We didn't tell you last night because we sent her home and thought it'd be the end of it and we thought telling you about it would just make you sad, for obvious reasons, but then she came back this morning with this.”

Rio stares at the screen in silence. She truly has no words. Not even in her wildest dreams (and she dreamt of Agatha a lot) she'd think Agatha would come after her, and then write her a letter when she couldn't find her.

“We didn't want to pry, so we didn't open it,” Jen says. “We figured you wouldn't want anyone else to know what's in here, even us.”

“Yeah. Thanks.”

“Rio, you don't need to read this,” Jen says firmly. “You don't need her.”

Rio shakes her head. “No. Send it. I want to know what she has to say to me.”

“Are you sure?”

No. Of course not.

“Yes. Of course.”

“Okay.”

“We'll try to get it to you as soon as possible,” Alice says. 

“Thank you.”

True to their promise, they mail the letter first thing in the morning and pay for the expensive same-day delivery. Later that night, Rio stares at the envelope for a long while before carefully opening it, trying to preserve as much of what Agatha had touched as possible.

 

My darling Rio,

I don't know where to start. I want to say that I'm sorry, but I know that you wouldn't believe me. I think you also wouldn't believe me if I told you that I loved you, and that I still do. So much. Which is the true reason why I was so angry when I found out about you and Wanda. I know I don't have the right to, but I can't (and never will) be able to stand the thought of you with someone else. I'm sorry for the way I treated you. You deserve to move on and find more love than what I'd ever be able to offer you.

I know that you won't forgive me. I don't think I deserve your forgiveness. I hurt you so much while we were together throughout this last year and even more when I let you go like that. I'm not asking you to understand why I did what I did, but I want to tell you that the way Ralph found out reminded me of the day I came home to Evanora holding the letter I had written to the girl I liked. I don't know if you remember me telling you about her, or about the things she did to me. I don't blame you if you tried to wipe every memory you had of me us. But that's why I didn't couldn't stand up for you. I saw young me all over again. I saw that horrible camp again. And worse, I saw my son being taken from me. I don't expect you to understand. That same night, you told me about how your mother always loved you for who you were, and I'll forever be glad you never had to experience or feel the things that I did. I hope you're doing okay. Maybe in another life, this ends differently for us. I wish you nothing but happiness and success in your career going forward.

With love,

Forever yours, in this life and in the next,

Yours,

Agatha

A.H.

 

Rio reads the letter over and over, only stopping when her tears have stained half the page, after which she sets it on fire and buries the ashes outside.

She regrets that two weeks later, when the TMZ releases an article about Agatha and Ralph renewing their vows. She knows then that the letter will be the last thing she'd ever get from Agatha, and now it's gone forever. She doesn't leave her room for a couple of days, and only eats when Hela brings food upstairs and pleads with her to love and take care of herself, even if it was just a little.

And so, Rio is extremely shocked when she wakes up a few weeks later to her phone buzzing with dozens of texts from Alice and Jen spamming the group chat with links to articles and tweets about Agatha's recent divorce. The first article that she opens reads,

 

BREAKING: AGATHA HARKNESS AND RALPH BOHNER DIVORCED

Published by TMZ

We have confirmed that the marriage between Agatha Harkness and entrepreneur Ralph Bohner has come to an end after a little over a decade. We haven't been able to reach them or their team as of the release of this article.
