“I'm not being cruel. I'm being honest. If you can't handle that, it's not my fault.”

Agatha wipes her eyes and sighs. “Forget it. I'll sleep on the couch and be gone before you wake up. And then you can pretend I was never here, if that's what will make you happy.”

What would make Rio happy is for her brain to stop sabotaging her so that she could just accept the fact that Agatha was truly hers now, no matter what happened in the past. 

She nods and buries herself further under her blankets, and stays there until she hears the door click shut.

 

Agatha isn't gone when Rio gives up on sleeping at five in the morning. Instead, she's laying completely still on the couch, and for a second Rio is scared she had frozen to death. With a sigh, she walks back to her room to retrieve some blankets and lays them over her. Agatha doesn't move, but Rio has learned to recognize when she's just pretending to be asleep, and this is one of these times. She watches Agatha for a while before padding quietly toward the kitchen.

She has been sitting at the table in complete silence for a while when Hela walks into the kitchen and places a hand on her shoulder before sitting next to her. 

“Hey,” she says softly. “Is there anything you want to tell me, nena?”

“I'm fine,” Rio says quickly.

“I didn't say you weren't.”

Rio shrugs. 

“I saw Agatha sleeping on the couch.”

She shrugs again. “Don't know why she's there. I let her stay in my room. Didn't even see her leave.”

“Rio, cariño.”

That prompts Rio to finally look at her. “Did you—um. Hear anything? Last night?”

“No. Should I have?”

Rio shakes her head quickly. 

“Why did Agatha miss her flight?” Hela asks softly.

“I don't know. She might have told me, but I wasn't paying attention.”

Hela sighs again and looks at her the same way that she did when Rio was a teenager and Hela had found out about something she had done and refused to tell her about. She stays quiet, just looking at her, until Rio's lower lip begins to quiver just before she breaks into a sob. Hela immediately pulls her close and strokes her hair.

“Shh, you're okay,” Hela coos. “You're okay. I'm here.”

She kisses the top of Rio's head when her shoulders begin to shake. Rio tries to say something, but she just sobs harder.

“Take your time.”

Eventually, Rio manages to tell her how Agatha followed her into the woods and how Agatha told her how she still loved her and how that ended with them getting in bed together, and then she tells her about how she forced herself to treat Agatha afterwards. Despite the fact Hela kept reassuring her that it was okay, she apologizes between almost every sentence, because Hela had worked so hard to help her heal, and she had thrown it all away.

“I'd never want to actually hurt her, mom,” Rio says through a sob. “I—I was just…”

“I know, nena. You were scared. You were being human.”

“But I shouldn't have—I just thought she was going to tell me it hadn't meant anything to her and so I thought I had to say it first, and—fuck, mom, the look on her face when I said all of these horrible things, I—”

“You were trying to protect yourself. There's absolutely nothing wrong with that.”

For some reason, that only makes Rio sob harder. She buries her face on her mother's shoulder and sits like that while Hela rests her chin on her head and strokes her hair for so long that Rio loses track of time.

Agatha stops dead in her tracks when she sees them as she walks into the kitchen. Rio probably wouldn't have noticed that she was there if Hela hadn't lifted her head. 

“I didn't mean to interrupt,” Agatha says quickly. “Sorry. I'll just call a car and—”

“No. You'll sit with us and you'll eat,” Hela says. “You have a long flight ahead of you.”

She wipes Rio's cheeks and gets up despite Rio's quiet whines and attempts to not let her leave. 

Agatha looks at Rio before shaking her head. “No. I don't want to intrude. This is your family and you're having a moment and I don't want to make things worse and—”

“Agatha,” Hela says firmly. “Sit. I'm not asking.”

“I don't want to make Rio uncomfortable.”

Rio sighs. “Sit down.”

“But—”

“Agatha, you're not going to make me any more uncomfortable than I already am. Just do as you're told.”

Agatha looks at her for a while longer before finally relenting. She doesn't look at either of them again until Hela slides a plate of toast and eggs toward her. As they eat in silence, both Rio and Agatha occasionally catch Hela glancing at them. When she finishes eating, Agatha takes her plate to the sink and washes it, still dead silent. 

“Thank you for feeding me. And for having me,” she says. “I really should get going now. I don't want to miss another flight.”

“I'll get my keys,” Hela says quickly.

“Absolutely not,” Agatha says. “Ill call an Uber.”

Hela shakes her head. “None of that. I'll drive you.”

“I—you really don't have to. I don't want to bother you.”

Rio sighs. “Mom. She doesn't want to. Just let her go already.”

Hela shoots her a look and Rio shrugs.

“I insist.” She stops by Agatha on her way out of the kitchen and whispers in her ear, “And I really want to speak to you in private.”

They don't dare look at each other while they're alone, nor when Hela comes back with the keys. 

“Goodbye, Rio,” Agatha says quietly.

Rio keeps her eyes fixed on her still half-full plate. Agatha sighs and quietly walks toward the door.

Hela puts a hand on Rio's shoulder. “I'll be back soon, okay? Go get that warmed up and try to finish your food. And don't worry about the dishes. Go get some sleep.”

Even though she's not going to do that, Rio nods. Hela kisses the top of her head and follows Agatha outside. 

The first few minutes of the drive are quiet except for the occasional drumming of Hela's fingers on the steering wheel.

“So, Agatha. What's your side of the story?” she eventually asks.

“My—what?”

“Rio told me hers. What's yours?”

“One second we were, you know…” Agatha shrugs. “And the other she was saying all these things about how she didn't mean for it to happen, and—”

“We'll get to that,” Hela cuts in. “I was asking you about the beginning. Did you always know you wanted her?”

“I—um. Yeah? Maybe?”

“Let me phrase this differently. Were you trying to seduce her the day that you hired her, and every day after? Because that's what it felt like to her.”

Agatha's head snaps toward her, and Hela keeps watching her silently from the corner of her eye.

“It's a yes or no question, Agatha. If you want me to help you, you need to help me.”

Agatha sighs. “Yes.”

Hela nods. “Okay.”

“I never meant for it to become as serious as it did,” Agatha says. “Like with every woman before her, I just wanted an escape from reality.”

“So you were planning on using my daughter.”

“What? No.” She swallows hard. “Well, I mean, yeah? But I'd never try to make her think that it meant anything. I'd always make it clear that it was just… I don't know. A distraction?”

“Well, Rio told me that she spoke with your ex-stylist before she began working for you and that she warned her about this exact kind of behavior.”

“It might have been like that with other women, but I swear on my life, Mrs. Vidal, from the second we started seeing each other, I knew she was going to be different. And I also swear that I'm not saying that just to get on your good side, and—”

“My daughter was twenty-five, Agatha,” Hela interrupts. “She was just trying to build a career doing what she loved. You were married. You had a kid. And you had an insane amount of power over her, and you knew that and took advantage of it.”

“I never meant to hurt her.”

“You did, though. Terribly.”

Agatha sighs and nods.

“You have no idea how scared I was when she texted me one night begging me to let her move back in with me because she didn't want to live in LA anymore,” Hela says. “And then when she got here she was terrified to tell me about what happened. You destroyed my daughter so much that she thought I'd hate and blame her for your affair, because she convinced herself that it was solely her fault.”

“I'm sorry.”

“Is that all you have to say for yourself, after everything I said to you?”

“No.”

Hela raises an eyebrow. “Go on, then.”

“I never meant to not protect her when it came down to it,” Agatha says. “I was going to get a divorce. I was never lying when I told her that. But I also needed to protect my son, and if I didn't do whatever Ralph wanted me to do, I'd lose him. God, Hela, I'd never be able to live if I lost my baby.”

“And then you almost made me lose mine.”

“I know. And I regret it every second of every day.”

“Rio came here so she could start fresh without you. She came here to meet new people and try to fall in love again after you ruined it for her. And then you showed up again like nothing happened.”

Instead of trying to defend herself, Agatha says, “I wasn't exactly expecting her to wait for me, but knowing that she's been seeing other people…”

That makes Hela chuckle. “She went on one date, Agatha. One that she wanted to cancel multiple times, but I insisted that she went because I couldn't take one more day of her just laying in bed crying because of how much she missed you.”

“She—what? She told me that she—”

“Rio says a lot of things.”

“I—um. I don't even know if she tells you about these things. I didn't exactly have the best relationship with my mother, and my only son is still just a child, so I don't know how these things work, and I'm definitely crossing a boundary, but—”

“You want to know if they had sex.”

Agatha nods. “I'm sorry. It's so weird and fucked up that I'm asking you that. Forget it.”

“Yes, they did. And then she came home and spent an hour crying on my lap because that girl just wasn't you,” she replies. “Don't tell her that I told you, though.”

“You're assuming that she'll want to see me again. She said that—”

“Again, Rio says a lot of things. And I'm not assuming. I know my daughter and I know that there's nothing in this world that my daughter wants more than to make things work with you.”

Agatha doesn't have an answer to that. Hela nods once.

“Why didn't you have a good relationship with your mother?” Hela asks. “If you want to tell me, of course.”

Agatha clears her throat. “For many reasons. Mainly because she sent me to conversion therapy.”

“I always told Rio I don't understand how some parents can just be like… that. I don't know why or how who your child loves could change how you see them.”

“She's so lucky to have you as her mom.”

Hela smiles. “I just want her to be happy, that's all.”

When Agatha doesn't say anything, Hela continues, “And you made her really happy, you know. She only told me about what you two had when she got here, but suddenly how happy she had been this past year and how she was always talking about how nice it was to work with you made sense.”

“I'm sorry,” she says. “I didn't mean for things to end the way they did.”

“I know you didn't, Agatha,” Hela says. “I'm not your enemy.”

“I know you're not. But I also know that I hurt your daughter terribly, and I'm sorry.”

“And I'm also not the one you should be apologizing to.”

Agatha only nods, and they don't speak again for another few minutes. 

“Rio told me about her dad,” Agatha eventually says. “I'm so sorry.”

Hela nods with a small smile. “I don't think she's ever told anyone what happened. She still trusts you, you know. Despite everything.”

“And I don't deserve that.”

Hela chooses to ignore that. 

“My Steve was a rookie in the fire department when we met. He was dispatched to get my cat Victoria down from a tree in my backyard. She hissed and screamed at him the whole way down,” she chuckles. “He was all scratched up when he gave her back to me, but then he told me that it was worth it because I was the prettiest girl he had ever seen.”

Agatha smiles. “And then?”

“And then he asked me out, and I immediately said no. God, I still remember the look on his face,” she says, wiping a few tears.

“It's okay,” Agatha says. “You don't have to talk about it if you don't want to.”

“Trust me, I'll talk about him every chance that I get,” she assures her. “Rio hasn't accepted that he's gone, even after all these years, so she doesn't really like it when people talk about him.”

“I can't even imagine what it felt like to her. I mean, I never met my dad, and me and my mother weren't on the best of terms, so…” She shakes her head. “I'm sorry. Keep talking.” 

“It's okay,” Hela reassures her. “You can talk too.”

“I'd rather let you talk.”

Hela nods. “He kept coming over to ‘see how the cat is doing’. Eventually, I realized he was the prettiest boy I had ever seen, too.”

“And that was when you let him finally take you out on a date?”

Hela laughs. “No. I made him work a little more. I didn't go out with him until Victoria finally let him touch her without hissing. So, like, six months after the tree episode?” 

Agatha snorts. “I don't blame you for wanting her approval. Did Rio tell you how she made me—God, forget it. Sorry.”

“Hold her spider?”

“Is this when you tell me that I'm not actually special and she makes every woman she meets hold her?”

Hela smiles and shakes her head. “No. That's when I tell you that you are very special, because she loves that thing so much that she rarely ever lets others handle her.”

“And then I fucked it up.”

Hela doesn't disagree. 

“Well, like what I was saying… we got married on the anniversary of our first date. I trained Victoria to carry a basket around her neck so she could bring us the rings, even if she hated every second of it.”

Hela is just finishing another story about Victoria, this time with a baby Rio in it, when they pull into the airport parking lot. 

“Here we are,” she says. 

Agatha sighs and takes a crumbled up piece of paper out of her jacket pocket. “Can you give this to her? It's my new address. Just in case she comes back to LA and still wants to see me.”

Hela takes it with a single nod and a smile, and Agatha pulls another piece of paper out of her other pocket. “And this is my phone number. Can you let me know how she's doing every now and then? Please?”

Again, Hela takes it. “I'll see what I can do.”

“Thank you for your kindness, Mrs. Vidal,” she says. “I'll truly never forget it.”

“Don't make me regret it.”

“I won't,” Agatha assures her. 

Hela watches her get her bags and walk into the airport before turning the car back on and driving back home. When she arrives, Rio is curled up on the couch, where she had stayed the whole time Hela was gone. It still smells strongly like Agatha, and she knows that it might be the last time she ever feels that scent. 

“Hey,” Hela says softly.

“Is she gone?”

Hela nods and sits down next to her, and Rio immediately scoots over to rest her head on her lap. 

“She loves you so much, nena,” she says, stroking Rio's hair. 

“How do you even know?”

“Because I was in the car with her for over an hour, and she kept finding ways to bring you up, no matter what we were talking about. She shouldn't have done what she did, ever, but I do believe her when she says she regrets it.”

“What if I don't believe her?”

“That's completely fine,” Hela assures her. “She hurt you a lot. It's completely fine if you can never bring yourself to forgive her, Rio.”

When Rio nods, Hela takes the piece of paper with Agatha's new address out of her pocket and hands it to her. 

“But if you ever do, I can assure you that she'll be waiting for you.”

That conversation is what makes Rio decide to leave Salem as soon as she is done with her commission. Hela keeps Agatha updated on how Rio is doing, though she doesn't tell her that Rio is coming back. 

A huge part of her feels like it wasn't right to leave her mother again, but Hela assures her over and over that she never expected Rio to stay that long and that she would always be just one phone call away. She cries for most of the flight home anyways. 

Rio doesn't tell Alice and Jen that she's on her way back until she lands in LA, and her first night back home is spent with multiple bottles of wine and the worst frozen pizza she had ever eaten, which made her wonder how the two of them survived grocery shopping without her for six months.

 

She shows up at Agatha's new address the following morning. When the door opens, she's surprised by Nicky at the door, looking so much bigger than the last time she saw him. She can't believe she missed six months of his childhood.

“MISS RIO?”

“Hey, sweetheart…”

“YOU'RE BACK?”

“I think I am,” she says softly. “Do you want to give me a—”

Nicky runs inside and yells, “MOM, MOM, MOM! MISS RIO IS HERE!”

No hugs, then. Rio's heart breaks a little.

He comes back a few seconds later, dragging Agatha by the hand. Once they're close enough to the door, he lets go of her hand and hugs Rio's legs tightly. She bends down to hug him properly and kisses the top of his head.

“I missed you so much,” he mutters into her shoulder. 

“I missed you too, buddy.” 

“Hey,” Agatha smiles. “Didn't know you were back in LA.”

“Hi, Agatha,” she says. “I'm so sorry for showing up here like this. I flew back last night, and—”

“It's fine,” Agatha immediately assures her. “Do you want to come in?”

Rio smiles and nods. When she lets go of Nicky, he immediately grabs her hand and drags her inside.

“I need to show you the Legos mommy gave me for Christmas!”

“Nicky,” Agatha cuts in. “Can you go play in your room for a little bit? Me and Rio need to talk in private. You can show her your Legos later, okay?”

He frowns. “Why?”

“Because me and Rio have some stuff to talk about, and it's only for grown-ups.”

“Why?”

“Because, Nicky.” 

“Why?”

“Hey. How about you go set up all of your Lego sets, and then we'll be all ready to play when me and your mom are done talking?”

“OKAY!”

He bolts back toward his room and closes the door behind him. 

“You're still good with him,” Agatha says.

“I only told him what he wanted to hear.”

Agatha nods with a small smile. “Do you want anything to eat or drink?”

“No. I just want to talk, if that's alright.”

“Of course it is.”

Rio sighs as they sit down on the couch. “The first thing I want to say is that I'm sorry for how I treated you in Salem.”

“You don't have to apologize for wanting to protect yourself,” Agatha says. “I understand.”

“It doesn't mean I should have done what I did.”

“I deserved it.”

That isn't a lie.

“And I did so much worse to you, Rio.”

That isn't a lie, either.

“And I'll hate myself every day for it.”

What Rio is gathering the courage to say is also far from being a lie. 

She takes a few deep breaths. “And I still love you. I never stopped. No matter how hard I tried to forget you, I kept wanting you and only you, and—fuck, I wanted to hate you. I tried to hate you.”

Agatha nods along, letting Rio keep talking at her pace.

“And I eventually realized that if we—if I didn't come home to try and fix us, I'd just spend the rest of my life looking for you in every woman that I met. And I didn't want that.”

“Rio…”

“And if you don't want me anymore after what I did, or if you think that this will never work out, it's completely—I mean, it's not fine, like, at all, but I understand, and—”

“Rio. Hey.”

Agatha gently reaches for her hand. Rio lets her take it.

“There's nothing in the world that I want more than to make things work with you,” Agatha says. “I swear, Rio.”

“Okay,” is all Rio can manage.

“We'll do this at your pace. You can push me away if you decide that's what's best for you. You can tell me every time something hurts you, and I'll change. If you think you need to teach me how to love you, I won't stop you. Ever.”

“Fuck, okay,” Rio breathes out. “I mean. Sorry. You're getting all sentimental, and—I just wasn't expecting you to forgive me like that.”

“I wasn't expecting you to forgive me either.”

“I wasn't expecting to forgive you,” she says. 

To that, Agatha chuckles. “Fair.”

They both jump when Nicky bursts out of his room.

“MISS RIO! I HAVE ALL MY SETS READY!”

She shrugs and smiles at Agatha, who laughs, and gets up to follow Nicky into his room. They both know they have to talk way more than this, but Rio desperately needs time to organize her thoughts.

She spends the rest of the day playing with Nicky in his room, and Agatha lets them be. When she forces herself to leave, Nicky hugs her tightly and tells her over and over how much he missed her.

“Are you going to start coming over again, Miss Rio?” he asks when they get to the front door.

“I—um. Maybe. We'll have to see,” she says softly. “I'll have to talk to your mom about it.”

“Mommy, can Miss Rio please start coming over again?”

“We'll have to see,” Agatha agrees.

Judging by the way they look at each other, though, it's very clear they've already decided.

 

And indeed, Rio starts coming over again.

After two weeks of daily visits, she starts spending more time at Agatha's than at her own apartment. She forces herself to set some boundaries, and Agatha puts a lot of effort in respecting them.

By the start of the third week, she's reading Nicky bedtime stories before leaving. A few days later, she lets Agatha kiss her again after Nicky went to sleep, and it feels so good, which is why it's not surprising that they end up in bed by the end of the fourth week after two bottles of wine. Rio lets Agatha take care of her afterwards this time, just for old time's sake. It's also not surprising that it keeps happening often after that.

A few weeks into that arrangement, Rio is awoken by a forty pounds figure jumping on top early in the morning. It takes her a few seconds to recover from nearly having a heart attack, but when she comes back to her senses, Nicky is sitting between her and Agatha and staring at her with a very excited look on his face while Agatha strokes his hair. 

“MISS RIO?”

“Hey, buddy…” she manages. “What are you doing up? It's really early.”

“What are you doing here?” he frowns. 

“We had a sleepover,” Agatha cuts in.

“A sleepover? And why wasn't I invited?”

“It was a special sleepover,” Rio says. She isn't sure if this is an appropriate explanation, but this is her first time being awoken by a kid when she's in said kid's mother's bed. 

“Yeah,” Agatha agrees. “For grown-ups.”

His frown deepens. “I didn't know adults had sleepovers.”

“Nicky, when two grown-ups like each other very much, they'll sometimes have these special sleepovers,” Agatha explains. “You'll understand when you're a grown-up.”

“But Miss Rio is a girl…”

“Nicky, honey,” Agatha says softly. “We've talked about this, remember? People can like whoever they want to like, no matter if they're a boy or a girl.”

“Yeah, but daddy said—”

“And we've also talked about how you shouldn't listen to your dad, remember?”

Rio snorts and Agatha nudges her foot under the blanket.

“Yeah…”

“Tell you what. How about me and Rio make you some pancakes, now that we're all awake?”

“YES!”

He jumps out of bed and bolts out of the room toward the kitchen. Rio can't think of many things she wants more than to make him breakfast every day for as long as he lets her. 

“Are you okay?” Agatha chuckles. “Jesus. I didn't think he would just show up here like that. He's been pretty good with staying in bed until I go get him.”

“There's a first time for everything,” Rio laughs. “But I don't mind. He's a good kid, Agatha.”

“He likes you a lot, you know. He asked about you all the time when you were gone.”

Rio wants to never leave so that Nicky will never have to wonder where she was again. 

Agatha kisses her softly on the lips and slides out of bed. “I'd get up if I were you, though. Wouldn't want to keep him waiting.”

A little less than an hour later, they're almost done with the dishes. Nicky is glued to Rio's legs, muttering something about how this is the best morning ever and how her pancakes were the best pancakes he had ever eaten, no offense to Agatha.

Agatha sighs when the doorbell rings. 

“Can you finish these?” she asks, wiping her hands down on a towel.

“Yeah, of course.”

“It's Ralph,” Agatha says. This morning had been so peaceful that Rio had completely forgotten about his existence, and Agatha clearly doesn't seem very excited about having to deal with him either.

Nicky lets go of her legs and bolts toward Ralph, jumping onto his arms. He gives Nicky a quick hug and a kiss and put him back down. Idiot. 

“Rio,” Ralph says with a single nod of his head.

“Ralph.”

They hold each other's gaze for a while. Ralph eventually sighs as he walks her, almost bumping into her shoulder.

“Are you ready to go, bud?”

Nicky nods. He turns back to Rio and runs toward her to hug her legs again. “Will you and mommy have another sleepover soon?”

Rio feels Ralph's eyes on her again. She looks at Agatha, who shrugs, which is bad, because she wants Agatha to tell him to not look at her like that. Or at all. “Um. We'll see. Maybe.”

Nicky grins excitedly and hugs her tighter. “Okay!”

He lets go of her legs and runs to hug Agatha, who picks him up and kisses him all over his face. Rio wonders if Nicky would let her do that too, though she knows Ralph would be very opposed to that idea. She hates how much she still cares about what he thinks. 

Agatha puts him back down and he runs toward Ralph, who waves them goodbye and walks Nicky out the door. As soon as the door clicks shut behind them, Rio turns toward Agatha with a scowl.

“You let him look at me like I don't belong here,” she says, crossing his arms. 

“Rio, come on. He was just surprised to see you here, that's all.”

“And that somehow justifies the way he looked at me?”

“I just think you might be reading into it too much, and—”

“Why are you defending him?”

“I'm not,” Agatha snaps.

“Yes, you are! I thought you'd stop pretending we're not a thing now that you got a divorce. But the second he walks through the door, you—”

“What did you want me to do? I barely talk to him as is. You expected me to start a fight in front of Nicky just because you didn't like the way Ralph was supposedly looking at you?”

“I'm not asking you to start a fight, I'm asking you to not let him treat me like that!”

“And do you think that wouldn't end up in a fight? God, Rio, come on. And he wasn't even—”

“Yes, he was!”

“Rio, I wouldn't be surprised if he and Jeff got married soon. He can't possibly be homophobic. He just didn't think you'd be here because last time I brought you up you were still living in Salem.”

Rio has to control herself not to ask why Agatha brought her up to him. Hopefully it involved wedding plans, though. Whatever. That doesn't matter right now.

“So what you're saying is that he wasn't actually being homophobic toward me when he found out about us and called me all those things?”

“No! I mean, yeah, maybe—fuck, Rio, come on. Are we seriously going to fight over how you think my ex-husband looked at you?”

“Oh my god, you're fucking unbelievable. You just don't ever change, do you?”

“Can we please not fight?” Agatha asks. “Jesus.”

“No. And you should do a quick Google search on internalized homophobia, you know,” Rio says with a shrug. “I'm surprised you've never heard of it, given that was the whole reason why you married him in the first place, and—”

“Don't you dare bring my past into this.”

“I wouldn't have to if your past wasn't haunting my fucking life all the time!”

Agatha shakes her head in disbelief. “He's the father of my child. Whether you—us—want it or not.”

“It doesn't mean you should let him treat me like shit!”

“God, Rio, come on. You were looking at him the exact same way.”

“So you do admit that he was looking at me differently.”

“Oh my god. Okay, fine, he was! Because he didn't expect to see you here, Rio! What's so hard for you to understand?”

“You're the one who doesn't understand me. You never have, and I'm convinced you never will.”

She brushes past Agatha and grabs her bag from the couch. 

“Rio,” she pleads. “Let's talk about this.”

Rio doesn't say a word as she leaves the apartment and slams the door shut behind her. 

 

Alice and Jen aren't home when she arrives, so she takes the opportunity to lock herself in her room without having to give them a rundown that would undoubtedly end in them insisting that Rio end things for real this time, because Agatha is clearly never going to change and Rio deserves and can get much better. It's bad enough that she keeps telling herself those things. She's unable to avoid that conversation later, though, and she was right about the results. She promises them she'll think about it, though they all know she won't. 

She wakes up early the following morning with a call from Hela. She has a slight feeling about what this call is about.

“Hey,” Hela says. “How have you and Agatha been doing?”

“Fine, I guess,” she says, just in case her feeling is wrong. 

Hela sighs. “She called me last night. Said you two had a bad fight.”

“Oh.”

“Do you want to talk about it?”

“She doesn't understand me, mom,” Rio says quietly. “You should've seen the way she let Ralph look at me.”

“Nena, if you ever have kids, you'll find that there's nothing in this world that a mother doesn't do for her baby and no situation where she won't put them first. Just because she protected Nicky before she protected you, it doesn't mean that she wouldn't protect you in another situation or that she doesn't love you enough.”

“Yeah, but—”

“She stayed married to a man she clearly hated so that she could keep her son,” Hela continues. “She'd do anything to keep and protect him.”

“I don't know what to do, mom. I love her. So much. But I don't want to spend the rest of my life living with the fear that she'll never stick up for me.”

“I do think you have to do what your heart tells you to do, but I also think you need to give her more time,” Hela says. “Which doesn't mean you don't have to tell her what bothers you. Just try doing it peacefully.”

“Maybe.”

“We'd do anything for you,” Jen says. “You know that.”

“Including sleeping in your disgusting bed,” Alice mutters.

Rio responds by kicking her foot and flipping them off on her way out of the apartment. She loves them so dearly, but that doesn't stop her from acknowledging that they need some serious help. Also, now that she has time to think about it, she realizes they didn't make one single complaint about sharing the bed. She'll make sure to tease them about that at a later date.

Rio calls Hela that night and they schedule the trip for the following weekend. She spends the next few days shopping for rings all across town and driving Alice and Jen absolutely crazy with all the pictures she sends until she finds the absolute perfect one the day before they're supposed to fly to Salem. It costs a lot of money, but there's nothing she wouldn't do for Agatha.

She makes it back to her apartment in record time after making the purchase, and once again finds Jen and Alice sitting a little too close to each other. It's not even remotely important right now, so she'll let them off the hook without teasing them for it this time.

“Okay, I finally picked the ring,” she says. “It's absolutely perfect. There's no way she isn't going to love it.”

“You better have,” Alice says. “I can't take one more day of you and Ag—I mean… you talking about Agatha. And rings for Agatha.”

Rio crosses her arms. “What were you about to say?”

“Nothing!” she says quickly. “See? It's so exhausting that I've been mixing up my words.”

Jen nods. “Mhm. Exhausting last few days!”

“I'm sorry,” Rio frowns. “As far as I know, you both have gotten leaves from work and have been doing nothing but looking at pictures I send you. Since when is that exhausting?”

“It just is!” Alice says. “You don't get it.”

“Right. What I'm thinking is, you've been busy doing something else behind my back,” Rio shrugs. “Or someone.”

“Will you ever stop talking about this?”

“Oh, so there is something to talk about!”

“No, there isn't!” they hiss in unison.

Rio throws up her hands. “I'm just trying to remind you it's completely legal.”

“Can we go back to talking about the ring, please?” Alice asks.

Rio laughs and pulls the box out of her pocket. She has to admit that although they're complete idiots, they are very cute.

“Okay. What do you think?” she asks, holding up the box. “It cost me a chunk of my savings, but…”

Rio can swear Alice and Jen exchange a weird look, but she shrugs it off. “So? Do you think she'll like it?”

“Um. Yeah. It's very… unique,” Jen says. “She'll love it.”

“Mhm! It's very Agatha,” Alice nods.

She frowns when she catches a glimpse of Jen kicking Alice's heel.

“Okay. You two definitely know something that I don't,” Rio says, setting the box down and crossing her arms. 

“We know Agatha definitely loves you so fucking much,” Alice says.

“But you obviously know that,” Jen nods.

Rio's frown deepens. “Right…”

“And you know her very well,” Alice continues. “And you know what she likes and what she doesn't like.”

Jen nods again. “And you're always telling us about it, so we're sure she's going to love it.”

“Like I said, it's very Agatha,” Alice says.

“Whatever that means…”

“It means that it's very Agatha, and that she's going to love it and you two will get married and live happily ever after,” Alice says, clapping her hands. “Now I think you should get going so you can pack your things. Don't you agree, Jen?”

Jen nods very quickly.

“Right. I just hope you two know you're not being subtle about whatever you have going on at all,” Rio snorts. “And please don't forget that we need you at Agatha's at ten tomorrow.”

“There is nothing going on,” Alice hisses.

“Nothing at all!” Jen nods.

Rio shakes her head and laughs as she heads toward the door. She wonders if there'll be enough time to set up a joint wedding by the time they finally admit that they have something going on.

The following morning, Nicky is ecstatic that he'll get to spend the weekend with two people who clearly know their dinosaurs. They still have to work a bit on their bunny knowledge, but Rio kindly sends them a few links to articles about Nicky's favorite bunny breeds.

When she's done, she goes back home, puts the box in her pocket and checks it about a dozen times to make sure the ring is still there.

The sun is setting by the time she guides Agatha down the trail with her hands over her eyes, hoping that Agatha can't feel how much her hands are shaking and her fingers are trembling. She can't believe she's actually doing this. 

“Where the hell are you taking me?” Agatha laughs.

“Somewhere you're really gonna like,” Rio smiles. “We're nearly there.”

She finally pulls her hands away when they reach the spot Rio had set up. Agatha gasps and she leans in to kiss the side of her head. 

“So? What do you think?”

“Oh my god,” Agatha says. “Did you do all this?”

“Um. The picnic and the candles, yeah… my mom planted the flowers and trees, though…”

Agatha starts to laugh and turns around to kiss Rio's face. “Did you know that you're the cutest, most adorable woman on earth?”

“Um…”

“What is all this for, though?” Agatha asks softly. “I thought we were just here for a weekend getaway.”

“Um. Well. I just—like, this is a special moment,” Rio says. Agatha raises an eyebrow and she immediately clears her throat. “I mean. Bringing you here is a special moment. And it had been a while since our last trip together, so it's even more special, and—”

Agatha laughs and kisses her to get her to stop rambling, thank god. 

“Alright, darling. What are the plans here?” Agatha says, pointing to the two canvases and painting materials on the corner of the picnic towel.

“Oh. Right. I thought, like, this is a beautiful place, right? And you're obviously even more beautiful, so I thought it'd be nice to draw a portrait of you, you know?” Rio says, trying not to stammer too much. “As a keepsake, you know? And then I thought maybe you'd like to paint a portrait of me too, so I bought extra materials just in case, and then we can show each other the final thing at the same time as a surprise, and—”

“Rio, baby, slow down,” Agatha laughs. “I think that's a wonderful idea. I just hope you know I'm a terrible artist, especially compared to you.”

“Right,” Rio says quickly. “I could teach you how to draw sometime. If you want.”

“If that's something you'd like to do, I'd love to,” Agatha smiles. She takes Rio's hand in hers and walks her to the towel and gently helps her down. “Why are you so nervous today?” 

“I'm not nervous!” Rio replies, shaking her head. “It's just… um. Like I said, I've been wanting to bring you here for quite a while. And, um…”

Agatha raises an eyebrow as she sits down in front of her. “Hm?”

“And also I'm not that good at painting on canvases either. I don't know why I—um. I mean. I think they'll be better keepsakes than, like, a regular piece of paper, you know? But I haven't painted on a canvas in a while, so maybe this was a bad idea because it'll look terrible, and—”

“And I haven't painted on a canvas since maybe third grade,” Agatha says. “I doubt yours will look even slightly bad, but even if they don't look good, why can't we hang them on our wall anyways?”

“Right,” Rio nods. “Maybe we should start?”

Agatha nods with a smile and Rio forces herself to take countless deep breaths to see if she can manage to stop rambling.

“You can, like, make a sketch using the pencil,” Rio says, pointing to the pencil. “And then you can, like, paint it using the paintbrush, and—”

“Alright, darling,” Agatha laughs. “Thank you.”

Drawing the sketch was supposed to be the easy part for Rio, but it's a little difficult with how much she has to concentrate on keeping her hands from shaking. Agatha seems to be struggling a little too, but Rio shrugs it off to the fact she didn't spend years of her life drawing human figures for school.

“How is it going over there?” Rio asks.

“I'm trying my best.”

“Um. Well, if you need any help, I can—”

Agatha raises an eyebrow. “I thought this was supposed to be a surprise.”

“Oh. Yeah. Right…” Rio stammers. “It is.”

Agatha laughs softly before turning her attention back to her painting. Rio decides to let her finish sketching too before picking up the paintbrush.

“If you need help mixing the colors, I—”

“Rio,” Agatha laughs.

“Right. A surprise…”

She wills herself to get back to her painting and mixing her own colors, which she messes up quite a few times before getting them right. This is definitely not one of her best works, but it'll have to do. The only thing she still has to do is write the question, but she can't bring herself to do that yet, so she stops to stare at Agatha for a while.

Agatha looks up and smiles. “What are you doing?”

“Looking at you,” she says. “Has anyone ever told you how beautiful you are?”

Agatha chuckles. “Right back at you, darling. I do think you're just trying to peek, though.”

Now that she's actually peeking, Rio has a feeling Agatha is scribbling something on the canvas. She frowns.

“No peeking,” Agatha chides. “I mean it.”

“I wasn't peeking,” she lies. “What are you writing?”

Agatha scoffs and shakes her head before returning to her painting without replying. 

“Um. Are you almost done?” 

Agatha nods slightly. “Just a few finishing touches, I think. Are you?”

Rio nods. “Yeah… um. I just need to, um…”

Agatha chuckles. “Okay, darling. I'll let you get back to it.”

Rio nods again and clears her throat as she tries to remember how to write. She manages to scribble a rather shaky ‘Will you marry me?’ at the bottom of the canvas, making sure that none of the letters cover Agatha's beautiful face.

“Okay. I'm done,” she blurts out. “Are you?”

Agatha smiles at her. “Well, I think it's as good as it gets.”

Rio swallows hard and nods. “Okay. On the count of three?”

“Sure, darling.”

And then Rio remembers that she should be filming this, because she's about to propose to Agatha Harkness, for fuck's sake. She quickly grabs her phone, opens the camera app, and sets the phone against a small rock.

“Okay.” She exhales. “Now we're ready.”

She takes one deep breath after each number, and she can hear Agatha chuckling quietly. With another deep breath, she turns her canvas toward Agatha, and Agatha does the same, though she's admittedly not very concerned about the quality of Agatha's work right now, so she doesn't really bother looking at it.

She instead scans Agatha's face, whose eyes have moved to Rio's canvas. She gasps in surprise, but then she bursts out laughing, so Rio is a little confused, and then she's really sad, because does this mean Agatha is going to say no and she's fucked this up terribly? 

Agatha is still laughing and seemingly unable to speak, so she forces herself to stop staring at Agatha to finally look down at her canvas, and—

Oh. Oh.

“Agatha, what the fuck?”

Agatha has not only painted the most beautiful portrait Rio has ever seen, but she's also written ‘Will you marry me?’ at the top.

Agatha finally stops laughing as she reaches for the box in her back pocket and opens it. “I'm guessing the answer is yes, since you asked me too?”

Rio stares at the ring for a few seconds, completely dumbfounded. She's not only shocked Agatha proposed to her at the same time she did, but she's now found out that she also went and bought the exact same ring as Rio.

“Agatha, seriously, what the fuck?” Rio chuckles.

She then realizes this might hurt Agatha's feelings, so she reaches for her own box and opens it, which makes Agatha burst out laughing again. 

“Oh my god,” Agatha laughs. “This is definitely not how I thought this was going to go.”

Rio is still in complete shock when Agatha drops her canvas and box and scoots over to her. She carefully slips one ring into her finger before reaching for one from Rio's box and slipping it along with the other one. Rio is still in shock, so she carefully turns Rio's hand over and places another pair onto her palm.

When Rio comes back to earth a little, she clumsily slips the pair into Agatha's finger, still in complete disbelief that this is actually happening.

Agatha giggles and leans in to hug and kiss her, starting with her lips and then moving to kiss her all over her face until Rio starts laughing in earnest, too. When she returns to her mouth and deepens the kiss, Rio realizes that it's probably about time to stop recording, so she blindly reaches for her phone and presses the stop button before climbing onto her lap and deepens the kiss further.

“We're getting married,” she breathes out. “Oh my god, I am getting married to Agatha fucking Harkness.”

Agatha laughs against her lips and nods. “We are, yes.”

She kisses Agatha again and they stay like that for a while, and Rio truly feels like they're the only people existing in this world right now.

“Wait,” she says, a little breathless when they pull apart again. “I have a question.”

“Hm?”

“Like, when and how do we start planning? I've never gotten married before.”

Agatha starts laughing again and buries her face on Rio's shoulder. 

“What?” Rio pouts. “It's a serious question.”

“I mean, I really do hope you're not hiding a secret wife from me,” Agatha chuckles. “But we plan when and however you want, darling.”

“Okay,” Rio says. “Here.”

“Hm?”

“I wanna get married here,” she clarifies. “If that's okay.”

“That's more than okay,” Agatha smiles. “Perfect, actually.”

“And maybe during winter? I've always dreamed of going to Switzerland for my honeymoon, but I think I'd prefer it with the snow.”

“That's also perfect.”

“And for the rings, there's this lady a few houses down who's very good with her jewelry,” Rio says. “She helped me a lot with getting them on some of my commissions when I was younger. Maybe I can ask her if she can mend our rings together?”

Agatha smiles. “I think that's a beautiful idea, baby.”

“Okay,” Rio nods. “I'll call her first thing in the morning. Or whenever the shock wears off, I guess.”

“You can take your time, darling. That's okay.”

“Alright. Um. Also, speaking of me making things, I was thinking I could make our dresses from scratch,” she says. “Unless you'd rather buy those expensive and fancy designer ones, which would be completely fine, and—”

“Rio, why would I do that when you are my favorite designer in the world?” Agatha chuckles. “Of course I'd love to have you make our dresses.”

“I am?”

“I thought I made that very clear when I had you make stuff for me for a whole year,” Agatha laughs.

“Okay. But you're not going to see them until they're, like, at least halfway done.”

Agatha frowns. “What? Why?”

“Because I want them to be a surprise,” she shrugs. Agatha still doesn't look convinced, so she rolls her eyes. “Please?”

“Fine,” Agatha smiles. “I'll trust you.” 

Rio beams and leans in to kiss her. None of this feels real. When Agatha pulls away, she strokes Rio's hair and leans back in to kiss the tip of her nose.

“That's most of the planning done,” Agatha laughs, moving to stroke Rio's cheeks and wipe her tears. 

Rio hadn't even realized she was crying, and she thinks that's at least part of the shock wearing off. She then notices that Agatha is crying too, so she also wipes the tears off her face with a slight chuckle.

“Alright, look. I'll give you the free will you want with the dress,” Agatha says. “But the spider is not dragging our rings down the aisle.”

“What?” 

“Hela told me about her cat and how she carried her wedding rings down the aisle. We are definitely not doing that with your spider.”

Rio pouts. “Aw. She'd look so fucking cute, though…”

“No, she would not.”

“Yes, she would! I could even make her a little dress, and—”

“Rio, no. Someone would step on her.”

“Is that the only reason why you don't want her there?”

“Well, that and the fact she's scary,” Agatha says. 

“She is not! Take that back.”

“Rio, she's a hairy ball with eight legs and eyes. You have to admit she's a little scary.”

“Well, dogs and cats are also hairy balls with legs and eyes and you don't call them scary.”

“Because they don't have eight legs and eyes,” Agatha frowns.

“Fine,” Rio says, rolling her eyes. “Can she at least be there?”

“That's something we can discuss,” Agatha replies, knowing damn well Rio won't take no for an answer. 

Rio smiles and leans in to kiss her lips again, and then begins trailing some more kisses all over her face. 

“You know she's going to be living with us now though, right?” Rio asks.  “I mean, we're going to be fully living together from now on, so…”

She laughs at how Agatha can't hold back a groan.

“Come on,” she says. “I know you like her, even if it's just a little.”

“I do like her,” Agatha admits. “What I don't like is the idea of having a spider in my room all the time.”

“You'll get used to her,” Rio smiles. “But I think we should probably head home. It's getting late, and I'm sure my mom is dying to hear how it went. And I also have some plans for us later.”

Agatha raises an eyebrow. “What kind of plans?”

“I booked this really nice restaurant,” Rio smiles. “And I figured you'd rather have the privacy of a hotel room tonight, so I booked one too.”

Agatha grins. “Well, I'm looking forward to that.”

She kisses her again before getting up from Agatha's lap and helping her up, kissing her hand and face as she does.

“Oh, god,” Rio groans. “And I have to tell Alice and Jen, too. I'm warning you right now that they're absolutely going to scream.”

“I can imagine,” she laughs.

To speed up that process a little, Rio sends them the video she took and just waits for the bomb to hit as they walk back home. Her phone rings a little over a minute later, and she braces for impact as she accepts the FaceTime call.

“Please don't scream,” she pleads.

As expected, the two of them immediately begin to scream, so Rio rolls her eyes and keeps the call muted until it looks like Jen is actually saying something. 

“Sorry, couldn't hear you. Say it again?”

“We knew,” Jen says. “Like, we knew before you told us you were going to propose.”

“BUT CONGRATULATIONS!” Alice screams from the corner of the screen.

Agatha snorts and Rio turns to look at her with a frown.

“Excuse me? How the fuck did you know?”

“Because your soon-to-be-wife came to us asking for help doing the exact same thing as you,” Alice says, rolling her eyes. “You two were a total pain in our asses for days.”

“And none of you thought to tell me?”

“That would ruin the whole thing, genius,” Alice mutters.

“Besides, do you have any idea how fucking funny and adorable it was seeing you two idiots planning the exact same thing at the exact same time with our help?”

Agatha laughs again, and Rio groans.

“Are you seriously going to side with them?” she hisses. “Why didn't you tell me that you were talking to them about this? Why aren't you pissed off that they didn't tell you I was talking to them about this?”

“She's still in shock,” Agatha chuckles. “I'm sure she's going to come to her senses soon.”

Rio shoots all three of them a look. “We'll be talking about this when I come back.”

“Sure we will, pornstar,” Alice says, and of course Rio decides to mute them again.

“They're a nightmare,” Rio rolls her eyes. “I fucking hate when she calls me that, especially in front of you, and she keeps doing it on purpose.”

“They were really helpful, though,” Agatha says. “I never thought they'd be willing to help me after everything that happened.”

“Yeah, and now I'm paying for their help,” she groans.

Agatha laughs and kisses the top of her head before taking her hand in hers to walk the rest of the path home.

“I think it's also about time you tell them they're about to officially lose two roommates,” Agatha says.

That was a fantastic idea, actually, and Rio was certain it was going to make them shut up about whatever they've been saying for the past minutes.

“Also, I'm moving out. Like, a hundred percent moving out,” Rio announces when she finally unmutes them as they reach the house. “It'd be really nice if you could start putting some of Vicky's stuff in boxes. With care.”

They seem more ecstatic about the fact that the ‘evil entity’ is leaving than they were about the fact Rio got engaged, so she flips them off as she hangs up. She has to admit she'll miss the feeling of living with them after all those years, no matter how much they piss her off. Chances are she wouldn't even have gotten engaged to Agatha if they hadn't helped, and maybe that's enough to make up for all the times they made her wish she had been homeschooled all her life so she didn't have to ever deal with them.

She finally pulls Agatha inside and drags her toward Hela, who's sitting on the couch with a very amused look on her face. Rio jumps up and down a little, trying to get her hand as still as possible so Hela can see their matching rings.

“We're getting married,” she squeaks. “She proposed too, and—”

Rio then realizes that Hela doesn't seem surprised by the matching rings or by the fact it had been a double proposal. She frowns.

“Wait. Did you also know?”

Agatha laughing behind her gives her a clear enough answer. She rolls her eyes. Of course. 

Hela laughs as she stands up. “Agatha called me some time ago to ask for your hand in marriage and for some help with getting everything set up. So yes, I also knew that she was also going to propose.”

“Mooooom,” Rio whines. “Why didn't you tell me? Why am I always the last one to find out about things?”

“That wasn't my secret to share,” Hela laughs. “Now, can I finally congratulate you?”

Rio nods, and Hela pulls them both into a tight hug. She can feel her mom's wet cheeks against hers, so she starts crying a little again, only stopping when Hela pulls away and kisses the top of her head.

“I'm so happy for you both,” Hela says, wiping the last of her tears as she steps away. “I know how happy you make each other, and I know you'll stay that way.”

They both nod, and Rio bites her lip to keep herself from crying again. 

“I'm so proud of you for not giving up on what you wanted, nena,” Hela continues. “And I'm so proud of you, Agatha, for setting your priorities straight.”

That makes Rio start crying again, so Agatha kisses her temple and pulls her closer.

“And I'm also so glad you both trusted me to help you with this,” Hela finishes. “Now, I know you have other plans that don't involve me being all cheesy and emotional, so as much as I'd love to keep going on and on about how happy I am for you both, I think you should get going.”

Fuck.

Rio checks the time on her phone and immediately panics. They are going to be so incredibly late. She takes Agatha's hand and starts dragging her toward the door.

“Mom, can you please call the fucking restaurant and tell them we're maybe going to be a little late?” she whines. “Fucking hell.”

“Watch your mouth,” Hela chides. “Yes, nena, I'll call the restaurant and tell them you won't be arriving on time.”

“THANK YOU!” she yells, already halfway out the door. “LOVE YOU, SEE YOU!”

By some miracle, they manage to not lose their reservation, and they proceed to have the best, most romantic candlelit dinner of Rio's life. 

After dinner, Rio drives them to the hotel she booked and Agatha spends many hours between her legs, taking her in about every possible area of the room. It's only fair that one of the first things she does in the morning, after getting fingered in bed and in the shower, is leaving the hotel a five-star review. She also makes a note to book it again for the night of their wedding, if they're willing to take her and Agatha as guests again.

They leave their rings for mending before they go back home, and Rio immediately misses the feeling of them on her fingers, and she knows she'll also be missing the feeling of them inside her when Agatha fucks her again. She absolutely cannot wait to get her hands on them again and then wear them for the rest of her life.

 

She moves all of her remaining things out of her shared apartment when they come back, and comes home to find out that Agatha had custom ordered a new, much bigger enclosure for Victoria for their arrival, having even already prepared it and decorated it with all of the things she knew Victoria liked. Rio liked to think that Agatha saw Victoria as her baby, too.

Their rings come in the mail two weeks later, and Rio swears to never take hers off ever again, and to also never allow Agatha to take hers off either. She spends most of the day admiring her finger and taking pictures showing her hand to post it everywhere, because she wants absolutely everyone to know that she, of all people, is getting married to Agatha Harkness. She hates the thought of Ralph possibly stalking her on the internet, but she wouldn't mind him seeing the pictures and deciding to lie on a train track.

 

They've been engaged for a little over a month when they finally sit down to tell Nicky. He knows that they both love each other very much and that Rio has officially moved in with them (and brought a really cool pet with her), but explaining to him that they're taking that love to a whole other level is obviously different. Nicky has never said anything to make them think he wouldn't like the whole idea, but they also have no way of knowing the kind of things Ralph has been saying about them when he's with him. 

“Okay, baby,” Agatha starts. “Me and Rio have something to tell you.”

“Am I getting a bunny?” he asks excitedly.

“Well, no, not yet,” Rio says. “Maybe for Christmas, buddy.”

“Oh…” he frowns. “Okay.”

Agatha reaches out to stroke his face. “Do you remember how me and daddy used to be married? You know, living together, raising you…”

Nicky nods.

“That's what me and Rio are going to do,” Agatha says softly. “Is that okay with you?”

Nicky's gaze shifts between the two of them a couple of times before he nods again, and Rio lets out a quiet sigh of relief.

“Yeah?”

Once again, he nods.

“Okay, baby,” Agatha whispers, stroking his hair. “We're really glad that you're okay with it. Your happiness matters just as much as ours does.”

He leans into Agatha's touch before pulling back a little and frowning. “But does that mean you and Rio will fight a lot, too? Like you and daddy did?”

In that moment, Rio swears to herself that she's going to spend the rest of their lives showing Nicky what a loving marriage was truly like. 

“No,” Rio says quickly. “No, buddy, no.”

“But if you're getting married…”

“Maybe we'll disagree sometimes, baby,” Agatha cuts in gently. “But Rio and I love each other so, so much. We'll always figure things out.”

“Okay,” he says. He stops to think for a while before continuing, “I have another question.”

“Shoot.”

“Now that you and mommy are getting married, do I still have to call you Miss?”

Agatha laughs softly next to her and Rio smiles. 

“You can call me whatever you want, Nicky.”

“Okay, cool!” He pauses for a little bit, thinking. “So do I just call you Rio?”

“If that's what you want,” she smiles.

Deep down, Rio is a little sad. She wishes Nicky would consider her to be his mom, too. Maybe one day. Hopefully one day.

“I think you'll really like the place we chose to get married at,” Rio says. “There's this really cool trail we can explore.”

“Are there bunnies there?”

“Well, I've never seen any, buddy,” she laughs. “But who knows? Maybe they'll come out once you're there.”

“Cool!” he says, seemingly very excited at the prospect. “Do you think they'll let me pet them?”

“Let's wait and see if any bunnies show up first, baby,” Agatha chuckles. “One step at a time.”

Nicky looks a bit disappointed, so Rio ruffles his hair and redirects him to the pile of dinosaur toys near him.

“We can also take those,” she says. “I'm sure they'll also have a lot of fun exploring the woods.”

Nicky smiles again and grabs some of the toys, handing them to Rio. 

“Can we scare your neighbors with them?” he asks. “Mommy never lets me scare ours.”

Rio looks up at Agatha, who gives her a pointed look that clearly means she's not allowed to let Nicky misbehave like that. She cares about making him happy, though, so she laughs and shrugs.

“I don't really have any neighbors close by, but I'm sure we can mess with my mom a little,” she says, winking at him.

“Nicky, do not listen to what she says. You are not going to be bothering anyone with your dinosaurs.”

“He's not going to bother my mom,” Rio whispers. “Let the kid have fun!”

“You are a terrible influence,” Agatha chides. 

Rio smiles and winks at Nicky again. “We better start planning, buddy.”

Agatha turns to check the time on her phone and sighs. Ralph was supposed to have been here almost an hour ago, and this has been happening more often than not lately on the rare occasions that he comes to see Nicky. Rio doesn't really mind though, both because she doesn't want to hear his voice or see his face and because she wants to spend time with Nicky. She doesn't like it when Ralph takes him away. Or when Ralph does things in general.

“Well, I'll go take a shower while your daddy doesn't get here.”

Rio smiles at her and nods. Nicky doesn't really seem to mind that she's gone, being more than happy to just keep spending time and playing with Rio. She wonders if that means that at least a small part of him sees him as his other mom.

A few minutes later, they're in the middle of a very interesting and intense dinosaur fight with his toys, but they get interrupted when the doorbell rings. Rio sighs when she hears the water is still running, which means she'll have to deal with Ralph by herself.

“Looks like your dad is here,” she says, and Nicky immediately runs toward the door. She takes as many deep breaths as she can as she follows him and unlocks the door.

“Hey, kid,” Ralph says, petting Nicky's hair when he hugs his legs. 

It pisses Rio off that he doesn't hug Nicky back. How dare he? 

“You're late,” Rio remarks. “Again.”

“Are you handling the handoffs now, too?” 

Rio fights back the urge to roll her eyes and pats Nicky on the back. “Go get your things and say bye-bye to mommy, buddy.”

Nicky's gaze shifts between them for a few seconds before he nods and runs down the hall. With Nicky out of sight, Ralph raises an eyebrow, still waiting for his answer. Asshole.

This time, Rio does roll her eyes. Quite dramatically, in fact. “When Agatha is busy, yes.”

“Well, you can tell her that handoffs are to be made strictly by the parents of the kid, as was agreed with our divorce lawyer.”
